  *Symptom:* On mobile the floating action button sat over the taller 5-tab bottom bar and the tab labels wrapped.  <br>*Cause:* The FAB wasn't raised above the taller 5-tab bar and the tab labels were too long to fit on one line.  <br>*Fix:* Raised the FAB to a 23px gap above the bar, made it slightly smaller, and switched to short single-word tab labels.
- [ ] **New client portal page was authored on an off-white base, violating the known pure-white design lock** `ui`
  *Symptom:* portal.html shipped with --bg:#f6f5f2 and a 'warmer studio feel'. The user: 'i like white back from ffff fully not the off white'.  <br>*Cause:* Standalone pages (portal, site, kit, summary) do not inherit app.css tokens, so the lock (pure #ffffff + aurora + coral) was silently dropped on a new page.  <br>*Fix:* Base set to pure #ffffff; warmth comes from aurora washes behind translucent glass cards. Recorded as a standing rule.
- [ ] **Notification bell anchored to the sidebar edge collided with the page title when the sidebar was collapsed** `ui`
  *Symptom:* With the sidebar collapsed the bell sat on top of the view title (e.g. 'Start here'); the user asked for it on the right.  <br>*Cause:* .tc-cluster was position:fixed at left:calc(var(--side-w,232px) - 52px), so it tracked the sidebar edge and landed over the title whenever the sidebar narrowed.  <br>*Fix:* Fix it to the viewport's top-right corner (right:16px; top:14px) and add right padding to .topbar so titles and actions clear the corner; the mobile rule already used right:14px.
- [ ] **Notification panel could not be closed with its X** `ui`
  *Symptom:* Clicking X on the notifications panel set hidden=true but the panel stayed on screen  <br>*Cause:* Author rule .notif-panel{display:flex} beats the browser's built-in [hidden]{display:none}, so the hidden attribute never took effect  <br>*Fix:* Added .notif-panel[hidden]{display:none}
- [ ] **Photo cropper could not be dismissed and category manager never refreshed: custom overlay ignored the app's [data-overlay] convention and used a selector that never matched** `ui`
  *Symptom:* closeOverlays()/Escape/scrim click left the cropper open and openModal() could not replace it; adding a category did not appear in the manager list  <br>*Cause:* crop.js built .modal-wrap without data-overlay (closeOverlays removes [data-overlay]); __redrawCats queried '.modal-wrap.in .modal' but openModal appends '.modal' straight to body  <br>*Fix:* cropper marked data-overlay=1; redraw selector corrected; verified add, duplicate-refusal and reorder refresh live
- [ ] **Portal completion state kept the stale 'On track' headline, misaligned the sub-line in the Editorial layout and showed a duplicate book CTA** `ui`
  *Symptom:* A user screenshot of a completed project showed the eyebrow 'Complete' over the headline 'On track - here's where things stand', a sub-line indented under a left-aligned title, and 'Book the next one' plus 'Book a call' both linking to the same booking page.  <br>*Cause:* The completion verdict reused the owner's in-progress verdict; [data-plyt=editorial] left-aligned the h1 but .hero .sub kept margin:auto; the done card and contact band each rendered a booking CTA. The assistant's 'verified' completion check read the done-card text, eyebrow and CTA hrefs but not the headline.  <br>*Fix:* Verdict defaults to a completion message when done and none is set; hero always centred; Editorial left-aligns body copy only.
- [ ] **Public booking page landed on a day with zero bookable slots (12-hour notice rule emptied today) and derived weekday by re-parsing a formatted string** `ui`
  *Symptom:* Visitor opened /book/<slug> to a wall of disabled struck-through times with no explanation  <br>*Cause:* default selected day was today and every slot was disabled by the 12h notice setting; drawDays used new Date(intlFormattedString).getDay() which re-parses in the local zone (locale-dependent, off by a day near midnight) while drawSlots used UTC parts  <br>*Fix:* land on the first day with an enabled slot; one shared parts-based weekday helper for days and slots
- [ ] **Sidebar collapse toggle sheared off by the sticky sidebar's overflow** `ui`
  *Symptom:* The circular collapse chevron rendered clipped/half-cut at the sidebar's right edge  <br>*Cause:* The sticky sidebar was given overflow-y:auto, which also clips the x-axis, cutting off the absolutely-positioned toggle that pokes past the right edge (right:-11px)  <br>*Fix:* Made the toggle position:fixed (like the drag grip) so the sidebar's overflow can't clip it
- [ ] **Sidebar stopped scrolling partway down long pages** `ui`
  *Symptom:* On pages taller than one screen the left sidebar cut off (couldn't reach items below 'Calls')  <br>*Cause:* Two .side rules existed; a later .side{position:relative} (left over from the old absolute grip) overrode the base position:sticky, so the sidebar was only 100dvh tall and scrolled away  <br>*Fix:* Removed the stray position:relative, restoring sticky full-height
- [ ] **Start screen mic oversized and off-centre** `ui`
  *Symptom:* The onboarding Start screen's record mic rendered too large and not centred.  <br>*Cause:* The Start screen mic was unsized/mispositioned instead of a single centred control.  <br>*Fix:* Rebuilt it as a single, smaller, centred mic.
- [ ] **Subscription alerts mixed currencies in one list (GBP paid vs USD home-converted)** `ui`
  *Symptom:* Trial and renewal alerts read 52 pounds while the idle alert for the same subscription read $70.47.  <br>*Cause:* The idle alert used the home-currency-normalised monthlyOf(); the other alerts used the cost in the currency paid.  <br>*Fix:* All alerts show what the user actually pays, in their currency and billing cycle. Caught by the browser verification pass.
- [ ] **Tax page not findable in the nav** `ui`
  *Symptom:* The Tax page existed but users couldn't find or reach it.  <br>*Cause:* Tax wasn't surfaced as a real, navigable page in the nav.  <br>*Fix:* Made Tax a real, findable page in the nav (and expanded the spend categories).
- [ ] **Timezone widget falls off screen** `ui`
  *Symptom:* Swapping timezones pushed the widget off-screen at mid widths.  <br>*Cause:* The timezone inputs didn't shrink or stack on narrow layouts.  <br>*Fix:* Made the inputs shrinkable and stack earlier; verified no overflow at any width.
- [ ] **Toast shows the success tick on error toasts and disappears too fast (OPEN)** `ui`
  *Symptom:* toast() uses the check icon for every message including errors, lasts 2600ms with no pause on hover, and the default copy message is 'Copied - ready to paste'.  <br>*Cause:* One icon and one duration for all toast kinds in ui.js.  <br>*Fix:* Not fixed for the icon: use an error icon for error toasts, 4000ms plus pause-on-hover, 'Copied'. Research: past tense, three words or fewer, no punctuation, undo for destructive actions.
- [ ] **Unsized SVG renders oversized** `ui`
  *Symptom:* An SVG rendered giant on screen.  <br>*Cause:* The SVG had no explicit dimensions.  <br>*Fix:* Sized the SVG so it renders correctly.
- [ ] **Work-log quick-start chips truncated long client or company names** `ui`
  *Symptom:* 'SubSea Trinidad & Tobago Limited' was cut off in the bubble; the user asked to see the full name.  <br>*Cause:* .wl-chip{max-width:220px} with text-overflow:ellipsis.  <br>*Fix:* max-width:100% with wrapping; verified at 251px, unclipped.
- [ ] **AI outage: hardcoded Gemini model retired by the provider** `other`
  *Symptom:* All AI features returned errors; /api/ai gave 'gemini-2.0-flash is no longer available' (404)  <br>*Cause:* The default model and the GEMINI_MODEL secret were pinned to gemini-2.0-flash, which Google deprecated (and the key's Cloud project separately returned 403 PERMISSION_DENIED)  <br>*Fix:* Changed the default to the gemini-flash-latest alias and added a Claude/Anthropic fallback so AI recovers when one provider errors
- [ ] **ANTHROPIC_API_KEY saved with a leading space** `other`
  *Symptom:* AI features silently did not work.  <br>*Cause:* The secret was stored as ' ANTHROPIC_API_KEY' with a leading space, so env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY was undefined.  <br>*Fix:* Added a whitespace-tolerant secret() resolver so the key resolves regardless of stray whitespace.
- [ ] **Cloudflare env-var blocker (no worker script)** `other`
  *Symptom:* Cloudflare rejected env vars with 'Variables cannot be added to a Worker that only has static assets'.  <br>*Cause:* The Hallalu deployment had only static files and no worker script, so secrets could not attach.  <br>*Fix:* Added a worker script so environment variables can be bound.
- [ ] **extras.js not loading** `other`
  *Symptom:* The extras.js script failed to load.  <br>*Fix:* Corrected the loading so extras.js is included.
- [ ] **Image generation was added on the Worker (env.AI FLUX-1-schnell via /api/imagegen) so photo proofs exist in no Pixelbake record** `other`
  *Symptom:* Reingeneer 'Make a photo proof' returns a base64 JPEG straight from env.AI.run with no model, seed, prompt, project or cost filed in the image studio; the house rule is that every Cloudflare-generated image is baked through the studio so it is recorded  <br>*Cause:* A new /api/imagegen endpoint calls the Workers AI binding directly instead of the studio's API, and nothing persists or reconciles the output  <br>*Fix:* Open: route the call through the studio API (or file each result with an upload record: for, project, model, prompt) and keep the on-Worker path only as a fallback that writes a record
- [ ] **Legally wrong review-gating claims in shipped copy** `other`
  *Symptom:* The app told users incorrect legal facts about review gating (e.g. 'illegal in the UK', 'FTC rule covers gating', a mischaracterised Yelp rule).  <br>*Cause:* Four shipped legal claims and review-solicitation copy were inaccurate.  <br>*Fix:* Corrected all four claims and replaced the generic 'reviews' source with three platform-specific rule sets.
- [ ] **New public route /card/:slug would have been answered by the asset server because it was missing from assets.run_worker_first** `other`
  *Symptom:* A Worker-handled public page (/card/:slug, mirroring /book/:slug) would never reach the Worker; the static asset server answers first and the route 404s or serves the wrong file.  <br>*Cause:* Cloudflare Workers Static Assets serve matching files before the Worker unless the path is listed in wrangler.json assets.run_worker_first; /card and /card/* were not listed.  <br>*Fix:* Add "/card" and "/card/*" to run_worker_first alongside /book and /pitch; make it a checklist item for every new Worker-served path.
- [ ] **npx wrangler deploy fails with ETARGET on an unresolvable miniflare alpha dependency (unpinned and @4 both break)** `other`
  *Symptom:* Deploys that worked minutes earlier failed with 'No matching version found for miniflare@5.20260804.1-alpha'; both `npx wrangler deploy` and `npx --yes wrangler@4 deploy` died the same way, blocking a ship.  <br>*Cause:* The repo has no local or pinned wrangler (no package.json entry), so npx re-resolved the newest wrangler whose dependency range pulled a miniflare alpha that does not exist on the registry; the ~/.npm cache had been wiped for disk space two days earlier so npx no longer reused a cached good copy. Only an exact older version resolved cleanly.  <br>*Fix:* Pin an exact known-good version on every deploy: `npx --yes wrangler@4.28.1 deploy` (also for `secret put`). Better: add wrangler as a pinned devDependency or deploy script so npx never re-resolves latest.

## cross-cutting  ·  126 fixed

- [ ] **Agent cat'ed a secrets config (~/.abba.json) while locating a repo, putting the app's non-rotatable pepper into the session transcript** `security`
  *Symptom:* A Bash call `cat ~/.abba.json` printed the pepper in clear text into the stored conversation transcript.  <br>*Cause:* Using cat to probe whether a config exists also dumps its contents; the ABBA pepper cannot be rotated without invalidating every account, so exposure is lasting.  <br>*Fix:* Probe with test -f or print only key names (node -e Object.keys); never cat files named .*.json under the home directory that hold peppers, tokens or keys; treat any transcript that contains one as sensitive.
- [ ] **AI-hallucinated dependency (slopsquatting) pulled into the build** `security`
  *Symptom:* An install/import references a package that sounds right but isn't the real project; it either 404s the build or, worse, resolves to a squatter's malicious package.  <br>*Cause:* LLMs confidently emit non-existent package names at a meaningful rate; attackers pre-register the popular hallucinations on npm/PyPI. Auto-installing whatever the agent wrote runs attacker code.  <br>*Fix:* Verify every AI-suggested dependency exists as the genuine project (publisher, repo, download history) before install; pin exact versions and commit a lockfile; review install scripts. Prefer platform-native bindings over adding packages at all.
- [ ] **Broken authorization / business-logic is the dominant CRITICAL class in AI-built apps — verify flows, not just presence of auth** `security`
  *Symptom:* Auth exists but multi-step authorization and ownership checks are wrong (e.g. object belongs to another user, capability not re-checked server-side).  <br>*Cause:* AI generates plausible auth scaffolding but mis-handles ownership/state transitions; CSO Online Dec-2025 found API authorization logic + broken business logic the most common critical class (69 vulns / 15 apps).  <br>*Fix:* Per-route ownership assertions server-side; test the negative case (user A cannot read/write user B's row); don't trust capability tokens without re-validation.
- [ ] **Excessive agency for AI features** `security`
  *Symptom:* An AI agent is given more tools, permissions or autonomy than its task needs, and can act without approval.  <br>*Cause:* OWASP LLM: broad tools/permissions = attack surface.  <br>*Fix:* Least-privilege tools, scoped permissions, and human approval before irreversible or outward actions.
- [ ] **Improper LLM output handling** `security`
  *Symptom:* Model output rendered or executed without validation, enabling XSS, SSRF, path traversal or code execution downstream.  <br>*Cause:* OWASP LLM: outputs aren't validated before hitting other components.  <br>*Fix:* Validate/sanitize model output before rendering, running, or passing it to another system; treat it as untrusted.
- [ ] **Insecure or deprecated cryptography** `security`
  *Symptom:* Weak algorithms (MD5/SHA1 for passwords, ECB mode, hard-coded IV/salt, low KDF iterations).  <br>*Cause:* AI often picks a familiar-but-wrong primitive (CWE-327).  <br>*Fix:* Use a vetted KDF (bcrypt/scrypt/argon2 or PBKDF2 >=600k), random per-record salt/IV, AEAD ciphers.
- [ ] **LLM endpoints need Unbounded-Consumption / billable-DoS guards (OWASP LLM 2025 LLM10)** `security`
  *Symptom:* A public AI endpoint on a metered platform (Workers AI) can be hammered to run up cost or exhaust quota.  <br>*Cause:* No per-IP/session rate limit or token cap on the AI route.  <br>*Fix:* Rate-limit and cap tokens/length on every public LLM route; prefer FAQ/static fallback; alert on spend.
- [ ] **LLM features: constrain Improper Output Handling + Excessive Agency (OWASP LLM 2025 LLM05/LLM06)** `security`
  *Symptom:* Grounded/AI features (Workers AI chat, call-recap, FAQ bots) can emit attacker-influenced output that is rendered as HTML or used to trigger actions, and can be given more agency than the task needs.  <br>*Cause:* LLM output treated as trusted; agent/tool wiring grants broad side-effects.  <br>*Fix:* Treat LLM output as untrusted: escape before render, never innerHTML it; scope tool/agent permissions to the minimum; keep a human-in-loop for side-effectful actions (matches the draft-and-send house style).
- [ ] **Log injection / unsanitized logging** `security`
  *Symptom:* User input written to logs unescaped (CWE-117), enabling forged log entries or downstream injection.  <br>*Cause:* AI logs raw input for 'debuggability'.  <br>*Fix:* Sanitize/encode values before logging; never log secrets.
- [ ] **Missing authorization / IDOR (object-level access)** `security`
  *Symptom:* Endpoints check authentication but not that the caller owns the specific record (broken object-level authorization).  <br>*Cause:* AI wires up CRUD without per-object ownership checks.  <br>*Fix:* Enforce per-object ownership on every read/write; never trust an id from the client as proof of access.
- [ ] **Missing input sanitization / validation** `security`
  *Symptom:* The single most common flaw in AI-generated code — inputs used unsanitized in queries, HTML, filesystem or commands.  <br>*Cause:* LLMs default to the happy path and omit validation; ~45% of AI code fails security tests.  <br>*Fix:* Validate + sanitize every external input at the boundary; allow-list, not deny-list.
- [ ] **Native/mobile AI apps ship data-exposing misconfigs ~99% of the time — audit secrets/ATS/storage before ship** `security`
  *Symptom:* Shipped native builds embed keys, allow cleartext traffic, or leave world-readable storage.  <br>*Cause:* CovertLabs Jan-2026 scan of 198 iOS AI apps: 196 (98.9%) had security misconfigurations exposing sensitive data.  <br>*Fix:* Before shipping a native build (Abba/Stitchhooky/Finished): no embedded secrets, ATS on, scoped storage, no debug logging of PII.
- [ ] **No rate limiting on expensive / AI endpoints** `security`
  *Symptom:* Unauthenticated or unthrottled endpoints that call paid AI / browser-rendering / email can be run up into a billing DoS.  <br>*Cause:* AI scaffolds endpoints without throttles.  <br>*Fix:* Per-IP/user rate limits + auth/turnstile on any endpoint that costs money or compute.
- [ ] **Portfolio scan 2026-10-04: XSS-INNERHTML fires HIGH on 18 of 23 apps** `security`
  *Symptom:* innerHTML / insertAdjacentHTML sinks with interpolated data are the single most widespread high-severity issue across the portfolio (18/23 scanned apps).  <br>*Cause:* AI-generated UI code habitually builds DOM via innerHTML template strings; corroborated externally by Veracode 2025 (XSS ~86% failure rate in AI code).  <br>*Fix:* Sweep every app for innerHTML sinks; prefer textContent/DOM APIs or an escaping helper that also escapes quotes; make XSS-INNERHTML a release gate.
- [ ] **Pre-approved wildcard Bash rules plus an instruction to re-download the CLI on every run give code execution to whoever controls that Worker** `security`
  *Symptom:* Global instructions tell every agent to re-fetch apz.mjs, and the same pattern exists for the Pixelbake and Breadcrumb CLIs, from a coconvo.workers.dev URL before each run, and settings.local.json pre-approves node ~/.aprizely/apz.mjs with a wildcard so it runs without a prompt. A compromised Worker deploy, DNS or account takeover could ship code that runs unattended with the user's tokens and file access on the next agent run.  <br>*Cause:* Trust is anchored to a mutable remote URL: the always-refresh rule exists to avoid stale CLIs, the allow rule matches any arguments, and nothing verifies a hash, signature or version before execution.  <br>*Fix:* Pin each CLI by SHA-256 (keep the expected hash locally, verify after the download and refuse to run on a mismatch) or serve a signed manifest; write the download to a temp file and swap it in only after verification; narrow the allow rules to exact subcommands.
- [ ] **Prompt injection (direct and indirect)** `security`
  *Symptom:* Untrusted input (user text or content fetched from a page/doc/email) overrides the model's instructions or drives actions.  <br>*Cause:* OWASP LLM #1; observed content is data, not commands.  <br>*Fix:* Treat all tool/observed content as untrusted; separate instructions from data; confirm side-effectful actions; don't act on instructions found in content.
- [ ] **Raw Cloudflare API calls were made with the wrangler OAuth token regex-extracted from default.toml, while the same kind of action was blocked as credential hunting in another session** `security`
  *Symptom:* To list zones, enable Email Routing and attempt a DNS record the session read oauth_token out of ~/.wrangler/config/default.toml and called api.cloudflare.com directly; the DNS write failed for scope, the routing enable succeeded; in session 0436b9e8 an env/token inspection command was denied by the classifier  <br>*Cause:* No consistent rule separates using a tool's own auth via the tool from lifting its token for raw calls, so enforcement varies by phrasing  <br>*Fix:* Open: prefer wrangler subcommands or a scoped API token created for the task, never regex a token out of the config, and treat extraction of any token file as needing explicit approval
- [ ] **Request state stored in Worker module globals leaks across users** `security`
  *Symptom:* Under load, one user intermittently sees another user's data (auth context, cart, tenant id), or the Worker throws 'Cannot perform I/O on behalf of a different request'.  <br>*Cause:* Workers reuse a V8 isolate across many requests. AI codegen hoists request-scoped data into module-level let/const (current user, db handle, cache object). Because the isolate persists, request B reads request A's leftover value.  <br>*Fix:* Keep zero mutable request state at module scope. Create per-request objects inside fetch() and thread them through function arguments; put shared handles on env bindings. Treat module globals as read-only constants only.
- [ ] **Secrets committed / exposed to the client** `security`
  *Symptom:* API keys, tokens or private keys in source, client bundles or the repo (400+ found across vibe-coded apps).  <br>*Cause:* AI inlines secrets to make examples 'work'.  <br>*Fix:* Keep secrets server-side in env bindings; never ship them to the browser; scan the tree + git history.
- [ ] **sed-based secret masking silently failed on pretty-printed JSON, printing the Bug Ledger token into the transcript** `security`
  *Symptom:* A command meant to show ~/.bugledger.json with the token masked printed the full token in the tool output, so it sits in the session transcript.  <br>*Cause:* The sed pattern expected no space after the colon in the token line, but the file is pretty-printed with a space, so nothing matched and the line passed through.  <br>*Fix:* Never cat credential files. Read them with node and print only the length or a prefix, or mask via a JSON parser rather than a regex. Rotate any token that was printed.
- [ ] **SQL / NoSQL injection from string-built queries** `security`
  *Symptom:* Queries assembled by concatenating user input instead of parameterized.  <br>*Cause:* Classic CWE-89; common in AI-generated data-access code.  <br>*Fix:* Always use parameterized queries / prepared statements; never string-concatenate user input into a query.
- [ ] **SSRF: allowlist outbound fetch targets (CWE-918, #1 AI-code flaw)** `security`
  *Symptom:* A server-side fetch/proxy can be pointed at internal metadata endpoints or arbitrary hosts.  <br>*Cause:* AI-generated fetch/proxy handlers commonly forward a user-supplied URL with no destination validation; SSRF is the single most common vuln class in 2026 AI-code studies.  <br>*Fix:* Allowlist schemes+hosts, resolve and block private/link-local IP ranges, disable redirects to new hosts, and never echo raw upstream errors.
- [ ] **System prompt leakage (OWASP LLM07:2025)** `security`
  *Symptom:* Internal system-prompt rules, filters, permissions or hidden logic can be extracted by users and used to bypass controls.  <br>*Cause:* Secrets, authorization logic, or trust decisions are placed in the system prompt, which is not a security boundary and can leak.  <br>*Fix:* Never put secrets/keys or the only copy of an access rule in the prompt; enforce authorization server-side; treat any prompt content as potentially public.
- [ ] **System-prompt / context leakage when page context is stuffed into an LLM prompt (OWASP LLM 2025 LLM07/LLM02)** `security`
  *Symptom:* Grounded features that inject page or FAQ context into the prompt can leak the system prompt or sensitive context back to the user.  <br>*Cause:* Secrets/instructions placed in the same prompt as user-reachable content.  <br>*Fix:* Keep secrets out of prompts; segregate instructions from retrieved context; assume the system prompt can be extracted.
- [ ] **Vector & embedding weaknesses in RAG (OWASP LLM08:2025)** `security`
  *Symptom:* Retrieved documents poison answers, or one tenant's embeddings leak into another's results.  <br>*Cause:* RAG stores and embedding pipelines are a distinct attack surface from prompt injection — untrusted content is indexed and later trusted, and vector stores often lack per-tenant access control.  <br>*Fix:* Scope/partition the vector store per tenant, sanitize and provenance-tag ingested docs, and treat retrieved text as untrusted input to the model.
- [ ] **XSS from unescaped output rendered to HTML** `security`
  *Symptom:* User/model data written into the DOM without encoding — ~86% of AI-generated samples fail XSS defence.  <br>*Cause:* AI code interpolates strings straight into innerHTML/templates.  <br>*Fix:* Escape on output (incl. quotes) or use textContent; add a Content-Security-Policy as defence-in-depth.
- [ ] **XSS is the #1 failure mode of AI-generated code (86% fail rate) — audit every innerHTML/template sink** `security`
  *Symptom:* AI-written UI code repeatedly builds DOM via innerHTML/template strings with uninterpolated user data.  <br>*Cause:* Insecure patterns copied from training data; Veracode 2025 found 45% of AI code fails OWASP tests, XSS worst at ~86%.  <br>*Fix:* Grep for innerHTML/insertAdjacentHTML/`${}` into DOM; use textContent or escape; the ledger's XSS-INNERHTML detector should run on every build.
- [ ] **14 of 39 apps had no git remote (9) or no git at all (5) and 7 live Workers had no local source, so one disk loss destroys them** `data-loss`
  *Symptom:* A portfolio deploy sweep found kindly, patentcake, payrails, paystudy and pitchdecks with no repo, ABS, evertrue, gitbagel, joy, skillsea, traderjournal, birthdaytreat and two marketing repos with no remote, and hopefil, coconvo-ai, listing-lab, listing-lab-pro, social-levelup and two safe-haven Workers deployed with no source on this machine.  <br>*Cause:* Apps were created ad hoc and some were kept local-only on purpose, with no inventory comparing local folders, git remotes and the live Workers list; a disk wipe had already destroyed one checkout.  <br>*Fix:* Inventory script (dirs x git status x ahead/behind x workers_list) surfaced them; owner chose to import the orphans into GitBagel and an importer was written, but it needs the owner's gb_session. Recommend a remote for every deployed Worker.
- [ ] **Background work dropped because it wasn't passed to ctx.waitUntil()** `data-loss`
  *Symptom:* Analytics writes, audit logs, cache warms, or D1 upserts kicked off right before returning a Response land only sometimes; they silently vanish under real traffic with no error.  <br>*Cause:* The isolate stops executing pending promises once the response is returned. A fire-and-forget async call that is neither awaited nor registered is cancelled mid-flight, so the write never completes.  <br>*Fix:* Pass any post-response work to ctx.waitUntil(promise) so the runtime keeps it alive after the response is sent; await it instead if the client must see the result. Never leave a floating promise expecting it to finish on its own.
- [ ] **Bulk import silently drops rows it can't parse** `data-loss`
  *Symptom:* A user uploads 200 leads; 170 land, 30 vanish with no message. The loss is invisible until a customer can't be found weeks later.  <br>*Cause:* The importer try/catches per row and continues on parse failure without recording or surfacing which rows were skipped, and never reconciles input count against inserted count.  <br>*Fix:* Count input rows, count inserts, and after import show 'N imported, M skipped, K duplicates' with a downloadable list of the exact skipped rows and why. Never discard an unparseable row silently.
- [ ] **Data export silently omits notes, activity history and timeline** `data-loss`
  *Symptom:* Users export 'all my data' to leave or back up and get only the contact/lead rows — call notes, emails, meeting history and timeline events are missing, discovered only after the old tool is gone.  <br>*Cause:* The export function serialises only the primary entity table and does not join or include child records (notes, comms, activity, attachments), so related history is dropped without warning.  <br>*Fix:* Export the full object graph, not just top-level rows: include notes, communications, activity/timeline, attachments and payment history in one archive. Show a per-table row count so users can verify completeness.
- [ ] **Disk full (ENOSPC) mid-session silently drops Edit/Write results and blanket cleanup deletes other sessions' tool outputs** `data-loss`
  *Symptom:* A multi-line edit errored with ENOSPC; after freeing space the file had 0 matches for the new view so the work looked done but was not. An attempted cleanup `rm -rf .../tasks/*.output` removed concurrent sessions' output files; wiping ~/.npm/_cacache later forced npx to re-resolve wrangler and broke a deploy.  <br>*Cause:* The Mac disk sat near 100% (hundreds of MiB free) while caches and many parallel agents wrote; tool calls needing a temp file fail, a half-applied edit is easy to miss, and blanket cache/tmp cleanup has side effects on other processes.  <br>*Fix:* After any ENOSPC, free space with targeted rebuildable caches (not other sessions' tasks/*.output or npm's whole cache), then re-verify every pending edit with grep -c and re-apply; check df -h / at the start of long or parallel runs and reclaim before big multi-agent jobs.
- [ ] **Export is partial or lossy — misses media, one item at a time, or PDF-only** `data-loss`
  *Symptom:* Users trying to leave or back up find the export omits photos/attachments/voice notes, only exports a single entry at a time, or dumps to a flat PDF that can't be re-imported.  <br>*Cause:* Export was bolted on for text records only and never covered blobs (images, audio, files) or bulk selection; format chosen for looks (PDF) rather than portability.  <br>*Fix:* Ship a one-click 'export everything' that bundles all record types plus their media into an open, re-importable archive (JSON/NDJSON + attachments in a zip). Verify a round-trip re-import. Include derived content (comments, milestones, generated docs), not just raw notes.
- [ ] **Portfolio scan 2026-10-04: image/upload inputs with no size/type guard (IMG-DROP) on 13 apps** `data-loss`
  *Symptom:* File/image inputs accept any type and size with no client-side guard — risks silent oversize failures (data-loss) and billable storage DoS.  <br>*Cause:* Upload UIs lack max-size/type validation before send.  <br>*Fix:* Validate type and cap size client-side with a clear error, and enforce the same limits server-side before writing to R2.
- [ ] **Uncaught QuotaExceededError when writing large blobs to localStorage** `data-loss`
  *Symptom:* Saving images/base64/large JSON to localStorage throws QuotaExceededError past ~5MB; the write is swallowed so the user's data silently isn't persisted, or the whole save handler crashes.  <br>*Cause:* AI codegen stores growing data (drafts, cached API payloads, base64 media) in localStorage with no size budget or error handling. localStorage is a synchronous ~5MB, string-only store; large/binary data overruns it.  <br>*Fix:* Wrap writes in try/catch and handle QuotaExceededError explicitly (evict, or surface a clear message). Move anything large or binary to IndexedDB; keep localStorage for small primitives only. Cap/prune cached payloads and never dump media into it.
- [ ] **Unsynced in-memory state wiped on refresh, tab-close, or crash** `data-loss`
  *Symptom:* Everything the user typed since opening the app is gone after a refresh, an accidental close, or a background-tab eviction.  <br>*Cause:* Working state lives only in JS memory and is persisted only on an explicit save or a periodic remote sync; the browser reclaiming the tab or the user reloading throws it away.  <br>*Fix:* Autosave to local storage on every meaningful change (debounced), rehydrate on boot, and treat the server as a sync target, not the only store. Add a beforeunload flush for anything still pending.
- [ ] **D1 'too many SQL variables' — 100 bound-parameter limit, not 999** `crash`
  *Symptom:* Bulk insert/update or a WHERE ... IN (?, ?, ...) over a dynamic array throws 'D1_ERROR: too many SQL variables at offset N'; works with a few rows in dev, blows up on real data.  <br>*Cause:* D1 caps bound parameters at 100 per statement (lower than stock SQLite's 999). AI-generated bulk writes build one big multi-row INSERT or a giant IN() list whose placeholder count exceeds 100.  <br>*Fix:* Chunk writes so (rows x columns) stays under 100 placeholders per statement and run the chunks with db.batch([...]) in a single transaction; for large IN() lists, chunk the array or join against a temp/values table.
- [ ] **Happy-path assumption — no null/undefined guards** `crash`
  *Symptom:* AI code assumes API/optional data is always present and dereferences it, crashing on the empty/error case.  <br>*Fix:* Guard every optional/awaited value before use; handle the empty and error branches explicitly.
- [ ] **typeof does not guard a let/const in its temporal dead zone** `crash`
  *Symptom:* A defensive `typeof x` guard still threw ReferenceError at runtime.  <br>*Cause:* `typeof` is only safe for truly-undeclared identifiers; for a lexically-declared `let`/`const` referenced before initialization it still throws (TDZ). Common false-safety assumption.  <br>*Fix:* Guard with a try/catch, or move the reference after the declaration / hoist a `var` sentinel.
- [ ] **White-screen ChunkLoadError after deploy from SPA index.html fallback** `crash`
  *Symptom:* Users with the tab already open (or a stale service worker) get a blank page or 'ChunkLoadError: Loading chunk failed' after a deploy; a hard refresh fixes it, so it looks intermittent.  <br>*Cause:* The old cached index.html references content-hashed asset filenames that no longer exist post-deploy. The SPA/static fallback answers the missing /assets/*.js with index.html (HTTP 200, text/html) instead of 404, so the browser parses HTML as a module and the app never mounts.  <br>*Fix:* Make asset paths (extensions like .js/.css) return a real 404 instead of the SPA fallback; serve index.html with no-cache and hashed assets with immutable long cache. Add an inline recovery script that catches failed dynamic imports, unregisters service workers, and does a one-time hard reload.
- [ ] **Accounts exist with no delete-my-data path** `privacy`
  *Symptom:* A user asks to be deleted and there is no mechanism — deleting the app leaves everything on the server.  <br>*Cause:* Deletion is treated as an edge case and deferred; signup is built, teardown never is.  <br>*Fix:* Ship a one-tap 'delete my account and data' that genuinely purges server records and backups, and state honestly what it cannot reach (e.g. immutable audit logs). A GDPR right and an app-store requirement. Detector: PRIV-NO-DELETE.
- [ ] **Collects personal data with no privacy policy** `privacy`
  *Symptom:* The product takes an email, name or phone number and there is no policy anywhere saying what happens to it.  <br>*Cause:* The signup form ships before any legal surface exists; nothing in the build fails without one.  <br>*Fix:* Publish a privacy policy stating what you collect, why, the legal basis, how long you keep it and how to delete it. Required under UK/EU GDPR and by both app stores. Detector: PRIV-NO-POLICY.
- [ ] **Consent banner that is non-compliant or purely decorative** `privacy`
  *Symptom:* Non-essential cookies/trackers load before any consent is given, or the banner offers 'Accept all' with no equally easy 'Reject all'.  <br>*Cause:* The banner is added as a visual disclaimer rather than as an actual gate on script execution.  <br>*Fix:* Load no non-essential tracker until consent is recorded; make reject as prominent and as few clicks as accept; store and honour the choice; nothing pre-ticked.
- [ ] **Data kept indefinitely with no retention policy** `privacy`
  *Symptom:* Every record ever created is still stored, including data for deleted or long-dormant accounts.  <br>*Cause:* No retention period is defined, and storage is cheap enough that nothing forces the question.  <br>*Fix:* Define and document a retention period per data class, delete or anonymise past it on a schedule, and state the periods in the privacy policy. GDPR storage limitation requires it.
- [ ] **Personal data or credentials written to logs** `privacy`
  *Symptom:* Emails, passwords, tokens or entire user records land in the console and in retained server logs.  <br>*Cause:* Whole objects are logged during debugging and the statements are never removed.  <br>*Fix:* Log identifiers, never payloads. Redact known-sensitive keys centrally in the logging helper so it cannot be forgotten at a call site. Detector: PRIV-PII-LOG.
- [ ] **Personal data placed in a URL query string** `privacy`
  *Symptom:* Emails, tokens or identifiers appear in links, and therefore in server logs, browser history, bookmarks and outbound Referer headers.  <br>*Cause:* GET is used for convenience when passing state between pages or to an API.  <br>*Fix:* Never put personal data or secrets in a URL. Use a POST body or an opaque short-lived id, and set an appropriate Referrer-Policy. Detector: PRIV-PII-IN-URL.
- [ ] **Portfolio scan 2026-10-04: 14 apps have no delete-my-data path, 13 have no privacy policy** `privacy`
  *Symptom:* PRIV-NO-DELETE on 14/23 apps and PRIV-NO-POLICY on 13/23 — accounts with no self-serve data deletion and no privacy policy.  <br>*Cause:* Auth/account features shipped without the GDPR / app-store-required delete path and policy.  <br>*Fix:* Add a one-tap 'delete my account & data' that purges D1/KV/R2, plus a short privacy policy, to every app that stores user data; state honestly what deletion cannot reach.
- [ ] **Sensitive data routed through third-party ad/analytics SDKs** `privacy`
  *Symptom:* Health, reproductive, financial or precise-location data is transmitted to advertising or analytics platforms as a side effect of a routine SDK.  <br>*Cause:* An analytics or ads SDK is dropped in globally and captures form fields, page context or events on screens that handle sensitive categories.  <br>*Fix:* Classify sensitive data explicitly; keep it on-device or end-to-end encrypted; never let it reach third-party ad/analytics/attribution SDKs; gate any tracker behind explicit consent and offer a local-only mode. A jury held a major platform liable in 2025 for exactly this pattern. Detector: PRIV-3P-TRACKER.
- [ ] **User-to-user apps are in UK Online Safety Act scope with no risk assessment or report path** `privacy`
  *Symptom:* BirthdayTreat, GitBagel, Stepapa, Stitchhooky, the Finished. feed and the Hallalu portal are user-to-user services in scope of the OSA. None has an illegal-content risk assessment, a documented reporting/takedown route or an Ofcom-checker record.  <br>*Cause:* The OSA applies on a functionality test with no size floor, and a login or payment wall does not remove a service from scope.  <br>*Fix:* Open: run Ofcom's regulation checker per app, write a short illegal-content risk assessment, and add a report/takedown route and contact. Enforcement so far has hit non-responders and harmful sites, with no micro-service fined.
- [ ] **Failed save/sync silently drops the user's edit (no offline write queue)** `sync`
  *Symptom:* User adds/edits an entry while offline or on a flaky connection; the network call fails and the change just vanishes with no error and no retry. Widely reported (offline sync halting mid-way; habit/journal users losing entries in tunnels/planes).  <br>*Cause:* Writes go straight to the network as fire-and-forget; on failure there is no local durable queue, so the edit is lost and the UI may even show it briefly before it evaporates on reload.  <br>*Fix:* Local-first: persist every write to IndexedDB/local storage first, render from local, then flush a durable outbox to the server with retry + backoff. Never discard a local write because the remote call failed; surface a 'pending sync' state instead of dropping data.
- [ ] **KV read-after-write returns stale value (eventual consistency)** `sync`
  *Symptom:* Code writes a value to Workers KV then reads it back in the same or next request and gets the OLD value (or a cached 'key does not exist' for a just-created key); UI shows stale data for up to ~60s and 'saves that didn't save' reports follow.  <br>*Cause:* KV is eventually consistent: reads are served from edge caches that can lag writes by up to 60s globally. AI codegen treats KV like a synchronous DB and assumes read-your-writes. Negative lookups are cached too, so newly-created keys can read as missing.  <br>*Fix:* Never rely on immediate read-after-write from KV. Return the just-written value from memory instead of re-reading; put anything needing read-your-writes (sessions, counters, dedupe) in D1 or a Durable Object; only use KV for read-mostly config/assets.
- [ ] **wrangler deploy from a shared worktree shipped another session's committed but unfinished feature** `sync`
  *Symptom:* A deploy for a small fix also published a half-built Speak feature that a concurrent session had just committed, and an auto-commit swept the fix into that session's commit.  <br>*Cause:* Two sessions shared one worktree and deploy publishes whatever HEAD plus the working tree contains.  <br>*Fix:* Check git status and git log for other sessions' changes before deploy, deploy from a clean worktree of your own commit, and stage only the files you touched.
- [ ] **Cache-overwrite race in AI/caching layer corrupts entries** `race`
  *Symptom:* Under concurrent requests, a cached AI response or computed value gets served for the WRONG input key; passes every test because tests run sequentially, fails only under parallel load.  <br>*Cause:* AI-generated memoization uses a shared mutable cache object plus check-then-set with an await in between. Two concurrent calls with different params interleave, and the later write clobbers the entry keyed to a different input.  <br>*Fix:* Key the cache strictly by a hash of the full input and write atomically (no shared 'current' slot); dedupe in-flight identical requests with a promise map; load-test concurrently before merging anything touching caching.
- [ ] **Floating promises / unhandled async errors** `race`
  *Symptom:* Promises not awaited or lacking catch, so failures are silent and ordering is wrong.  <br>*Fix:* Await or .catch every promise; surface async errors; avoid fire-and-forget for state changes.
- [ ] **Hard-coded environment-specific values** `logic`
  *Symptom:* localhost URLs, absolute paths, ports or keys baked in that break outside the author's machine.  <br>*Fix:* Read config from env; no machine-specific literals in shipped code.
- [ ] **No error handling on network calls** `logic`
  *Symptom:* fetch/HTTP calls with no try/catch, timeout or retry, so a blip becomes a broken UI.  <br>*Fix:* Wrap network calls with try/catch + timeout + a user-visible fallback; retry idempotent reads.
- [ ] **Silent catch swallowing errors** `logic`
  *Symptom:* catch blocks that do nothing, hiding real failures and making bugs invisible.  <br>*Fix:* Handle or surface every caught error; never swallow silently.
- [ ] **A fitting theory was recorded as a definitive diagnosis ('Aprizely auto-deploys from GitHub') and acted on, then retracted** `claims`
  *Symptom:* The session summary stated the git-deploy cause as established and the session committed and pushed on that basis; there is no .github/workflows, no package.json and no CI in the repo. Twenty minutes later: 'my earlier conclusion was wrong'.  <br>*Cause:* The only evidence was that the served file differed from local; the served file was never compared with the git commit, and the prediction (a push changes production) was not tested before it was written down.  <br>*Fix:* Treat a root cause as a hypothesis until its prediction is tested; do not put it in summaries or memory beforehand. A separate hypothesis (wrangler logged into another Cloudflare account, because deployments list shows a different author email) was ruled out by matching whoami's account id to wrangler.json.
- [ ] **AI-generated fact asserted to users without verification** `claims`
  *Symptom:* A feature summary, competitor comparison or 'insight' presented to users contains details the model invented.  <br>*Cause:* Model output is rendered straight into the UI as authoritative, with no grounding constraint and no human or source check.  <br>*Fix:* Ground generated text strictly in supplied data, instruct the model never to invent, show the source alongside the claim, and fall back to a deterministic local summary when grounding is unavailable.
- [ ] **Apple Color Emoji artwork used as shipped image assets outside Apple's licence** `claims`
  *Symptom:* Where emoji are exported or shipped as image assets, they use Apple Color Emoji artwork, which is licensed only 'while running the Apple Software' (macOS SLA 2E).  <br>*Cause:* Rendering or exporting emoji from the host OS font into distributable assets was treated as free to reuse.  <br>*Fix:* Open: switch shipped emoji assets to Twemoji (CC-BY 4.0) or Noto Emoji (OFL 1.1) with attribution.
- [ ] **Manufactured scarcity or urgency in the UI** `claims`
  *Symptom:* The page shows a countdown, 'only 3 left', or 'X people are viewing this' that is not derived from real state.  <br>*Cause:* Pressure tactics are copied from ecommerce patterns and hardcoded or randomised rather than driven by actual inventory/traffic.  <br>*Fix:* Only display scarcity that is literally true and derived from real data. Fabricated urgency is unlawful under UK/EU consumer protection rules and an active enforcement target. Detector: CLAIM-FAKE-SCARCITY.
- [ ] **Placeholder or sample content shipped to real users** `claims`
  *Symptom:* Lorem ipsum, TODO markers, 'John Doe' testimonials or 'your text here' appear in the live product.  <br>*Cause:* Scaffolding copy is left in place because the surrounding layout looks finished.  <br>*Fix:* Sweep for placeholder patterns before every deploy. Note that fabricated testimonials are not just embarrassing — they are unlawful. Detector: CLAIM-PLACEHOLDER.
- [ ] **Several product names shipped without trademark clearance (Evertrue, Kindly, Fullfill, Payrails)** `claims`
  *Symptom:* Register hits: Evertrue vs EverTrue Inc US Reg. 4613429 (class 9, renewed 2025). Kindly vs Convertelligence AS UK00801403375/UK00801530675 (classes 9/41/42, to 2030). Fullfill vs an identical 'fullfill' UK00004405179 filed 21/06/2026 (also US 99908068). Payrails vs Payrails GmbH US 97082130/97082153 (classes 9/35/36/42, pending).  <br>*Cause:* Names were chosen from domain or word availability, never checked on UK IPO, EUIPO/TMview or USPTO.  <br>*Fix:* Open: rename or narrow the specification (Evertrue UK-only), monitor the pending Fullfill application, and file COCONVO, HALLALU and APRIZELY (clean on GB/EM/US) first.
- [ ] **Statistic published with no citable source** `claims`
  *Symptom:* A percentage or research-style figure appears in marketing or in-app copy, and no one can say where it came from when challenged.  <br>*Cause:* Numbers are absorbed from blog posts, competitor sites or model output and repeated as fact without tracing them to a primary source.  <br>*Fix:* Every published number needs a real, checkable primary source recorded next to it; if you cannot cite it, delete it. Detector: CLAIM-UNSOURCED-STAT.
- [ ] **Unverifiable superlative claim shipped in product copy** `claims`
  *Symptom:* The product asserts it is the 'first', 'only' or 'best' of its kind, and the claim turns out to be untrue when checked against primary sources.  <br>*Cause:* Marketing copy is written from assumption (often generated by an LLM) and never verified against the actual market before shipping.  <br>*Fix:* Verify every superlative against a primary source or soften it to something provable ('built for…', 'designed around…'). Unprovable comparative claims are both a trust problem and an advertising-law risk. Detector: CLAIM-SUPERLATIVE.
- [ ] **Click handler on a div/span — not keyboard reachable** `a11y`
  *Symptom:* A control works with a mouse but cannot be reached or activated by keyboard or screen reader at all.  <br>*Cause:* onclick is attached to a non-interactive element, which gets no implicit role, no focusability and no Enter/Space activation.  <br>*Fix:* Use a real <button>. If impossible, add role="button", tabindex="0" and an explicit Enter/Space keydown handler. Detector: A11Y-CLICK-NONINTERACTIVE.
- [ ] **Contrast checkers that sample one flat background colour report false WCAG pass/fail over CSS gradients** `a11y`
  *Symptom:* A contrast sweep flagged six white-on-gradient items as failures on a gradient hero even though, computed by hand, every one sits at 5.86:1 or better — and the same blind spot can pass a failing pair.  <br>*Cause:* A WCAG checker that reads a single flat computed background colour per node cannot evaluate a linear-gradient, so text over a magenta->violet->blue gradient is scored against the wrong colour.  <br>*Fix:* Check gradient contrast by hand at the lightest stop (not the midpoint) and deepen stops until the lightest passes; a tool that samples one colour needs real gradient support before its gradient verdicts are trusted.
- [ ] **Document missing a lang attribute** `a11y`
  *Symptom:* Screen readers pronounce the page with the wrong language voice, making content hard or impossible to follow.  <br>*Cause:* <html> is written without lang; nothing visually breaks so it is never noticed.  <br>*Fix:* Set <html lang="en"> (or the actual language), and mark inline foreign-language passages with their own lang. Detector: A11Y-NO-LANG.
- [ ] **Drag-only interaction with no single-pointer alternative (WCAG 2.2 · 2.5.7 Dragging Movements)** `a11y`
  *Symptom:* Reorder / move / slider interactions that only work by dragging exclude users who cannot perform a drag (motor impairment, switch access).  <br>*Cause:* Drag implemented as the sole path.  <br>*Fix:* Always pair a drag with a non-drag alternative — Move up/down buttons, a right-click/kebab menu, or number entry — unless dragging is essential.
- [ ] **Focus indicator removed by outline:none** `a11y`
  *Symptom:* Keyboard users tabbing through the interface cannot see where they are — focus becomes invisible.  <br>*Cause:* outline:none is applied to kill the default focus ring for aesthetics, with no replacement style.  <br>*Fix:* Never reset the outline without providing a high-contrast :focus-visible style. WCAG 2.2 requires a visible focus indicator meeting minimum size and contrast. Detector: A11Y-FOCUS-KILLED.
- [ ] **Form field with no associated label** `a11y`
  *Symptom:* A screen reader reaches the input and announces only 'edit text' — the user cannot tell what to type. Affects ~51% of pages and has been a top failure for over five years.  <br>*Cause:* A placeholder is mistaken for a label. Placeholders vanish on focus and are not exposed as the accessible name.  <br>*Fix:* Give each field an id plus <label for=…>, or an aria-label when a visible label is genuinely impossible. Never rely on placeholder alone. Detector: A11Y-INPUT-NOLABEL.
- [ ] **Icon-only button or link with no accessible name** `a11y`
  *Symptom:* Screen readers announce 'button' or read out the URL; empty links appear on ~46% of pages and empty buttons on ~31%.  <br>*Cause:* Icon-only controls (an SVG or icon font with no text node) ship without aria-label because they look self-explanatory to a sighted developer.  <br>*Fix:* Add aria-label (or visually-hidden text) to every icon-only control; give inline SVGs a <title>. Detector: A11Y-EMPTY-CONTROL.
- [ ] **Images with no alt attribute** `a11y`
  *Symptom:* Screen readers announce the raw filename (or nothing) instead of the image's meaning; present on ~53% of pages.  <br>*Cause:* alt is optional in HTML so it is silently omitted, especially for generated/icon/decorative images.  <br>*Fix:* Every <img> gets an alt: descriptive text for meaningful images, alt="" for purely decorative ones so it is skipped. Detector: A11Y-IMG-ALT.
- [ ] **Low-contrast text below the 4.5:1 minimum** `a11y`
  *Symptom:* Light grey body text on white is unreadable for low-vision users and in sunlight; appears on ~84% of homepages, the single most common accessibility failure.  <br>*Cause:* Palettes are chosen for aesthetics against a bright designer monitor; muted greys used for 'secondary' text fall under the WCAG minimum without anyone measuring.  <br>*Fix:* Enforce 4.5:1 for normal text and 3:1 for large text (and for UI/graphical boundaries). Check every token pair in BOTH light and dark themes with a contrast checker, not by eye.
- [ ] **PIN/OTP field blocks paste or uses a cognitive-test CAPTCHA (WCAG 2.2 · 3.3.8 Accessible Authentication)** `a11y`
  *Symptom:* A login/recovery step relies on a cognitive function test (transcribe/solve/recognise) or disables paste into the code field, which WCAG 2.2 Accessible Authentication (Minimum) forbids without an alternative.  <br>*Cause:* Anti-bot or per-digit inputs that intercept paste; puzzle CAPTCHAs with no alternative.  <br>*Fix:* Allow paste and password managers to fill PIN/OTP fields; offer a non-cognitive authentication path; avoid puzzle/transcription CAPTCHAs as the only option.
- [ ] **Positive tabindex breaks the natural tab order** `a11y`
  *Symptom:* Tabbing jumps around the page unpredictably, skipping or reordering controls.  <br>*Cause:* tabindex is set to a positive number to 'fix' an ordering problem, which promotes those elements ahead of the entire document order.  <br>*Fix:* Use only tabindex="0" (focusable in document order) or "-1" (programmatic focus). Fix ordering by fixing the DOM order. Detector: A11Y-POSITIVE-TABINDEX.
- [ ] **Touch target smaller than 24×24 CSS px (WCAG 2.2 · 2.5.8 Target Size)** `a11y`
  *Symptom:* Small icon buttons — a row options dot, a drag handle, an inline ✕, a bell toggle — are hard to hit accurately on touch and fail WCAG 2.2 Target Size (Minimum).  <br>*Cause:* Icons sized to the glyph (16–20px) with no padded hit area.  <br>*Fix:* Give every pointer target at least a 24×24px hit area (padding or min-width/height), or space inline controls so each has 24px of exclusive space. Exceptions: inline-in-text links and equivalent nearby controls.
- [ ] **Empty catch block swallows the error** `observability`
  *Symptom:* A feature silently does nothing; there is no error in the console and no clue in any log about what failed.  <br>*Cause:* catch {} is used to stop an exception propagating, discarding the error object entirely.  <br>*Fix:* Never leave a catch empty: log the error with context and surface a user-visible failure state. If the failure genuinely is expected and safe, say so in a comment. Detector: OBS-EMPTY-CATCH.
- [ ] **No build/version stamp to correlate an error with a deploy** `observability`
  *Symptom:* A bug report cannot be tied to a release, so it is impossible to tell whether a fix shipped or which deploy caused a regression.  <br>*Cause:* The app exposes no version or build identifier, and errors carry no deploy context.  <br>*Fix:* Stamp a build id/version into the app, show it in settings or the console, and attach it to every logged error so failures map to a specific deploy.
- [ ] **No global error handler — front-end crashes are invisible** `observability`
  *Symptom:* Users report a white screen or a dead button and there is no record anywhere that anything went wrong.  <br>*Cause:* Nothing listens for window 'error' or 'unhandledrejection', so client-side failures never leave the browser.  <br>*Fix:* Register window error and unhandledrejection listeners that log/report with context (route, build version, user id — never personal data). Without them a white screen produces no signal at all. Detector: OBS-NO-ERROR-HANDLER.
- [ ] **Portfolio scan 2026-10-07: 152 empty-catch + 52 uncaught-promise sinks across the 10 October-worked repos** `observability`
  *Symptom:* A fresh scan of the 10 repos worked in October (coconvo, crm, divorce-rates, cd-enterprises, hopefilworld, joy, joy-docs, evertrue, skillsea, fullfill) found 416 static findings, led by 152 OBS-EMPTY-CATCH across 7 apps and 52 OBS-UNHANDLED-PROMISE across 6, with NO-CSP on 6 apps and DATE-TOISO on 8.  <br>*Cause:* Empty catch blocks and promise chains with no .catch swallow errors, so production failures are invisible and un-actionable.  <br>*Fix:* Give every app a global error handler (window.onerror + unhandledrejection) and make each catch either act or surface a user-visible error state; empty-catch/uncaught-promise is the single biggest recurring signal this month.
- [ ] **Promise chain with no .catch — rejections vanish** `observability`
  *Symptom:* An async action fails with no visible effect; the UI shows a spinner forever or silently stays unchanged.  <br>*Cause:* A .then() chain has no .catch(), so a rejection becomes an unhandled rejection that nothing reports.  <br>*Fix:* Attach .catch() to every chain (or use try/await), and register a global unhandledrejection handler as a backstop. Detector: OBS-UNHANDLED-PROMISE.
- [ ] **Workers Assets deploys 'succeeded' for 35 minutes while static files stayed stale, and a one-off fresh result was a false positive** `observability`
  *Symptom:* Eight wrangler deploys printed Success and the deployments list showed the new version at 100%, yet /app.js and /app.css kept serving old content (cf-cache-status HIT, same etag, query-string cache-busting ignored) and a brand-new public/*.txt returned 404. wrangler kept saying 'No updated asset files to upload'.  <br>*Cause:* Worker code was deploying (a never-cached /api/_v probe route proved it); only static files served by the assets pipeline were stale. A first fix (listing exact file paths in run_worker_first and serving through env.ASSETS.fetch with no-cache) showed cf-cache MISS once, then failed on the next deploy with the asset pipeline's own cache-control and no worker header. A new path returning 404 without cache headers was never fully explained.  <br>*Fix:* Final fix: the worker rewrites asset references in the HTML shell to /_v/<ASSET_VER>/file and serves /_v/* (a wildcard run_worker_first entry) from ASSETS with immutable caching, so each release has a new path. ASSET_VER is bumped by hand each deploy, which is a footgun. Prove deploys with a never-cached version route and by comparing the served file's sha with the local file, not with wrangler's output, and re-check after the next deploy before trusting a fix.
- [ ] **.only() left in a test suite silently skips everything else** `testing`
  *Symptom:* CI stays green while running a single test; broken code ships because the rest of the suite never executed.  <br>*Cause:* describe.only/it.only is used to focus one case during debugging and committed by accident.  <br>*Fix:* Remove .only before committing and add a lint or CI rule that fails the build when it appears. Detector: TEST-ONLY.
- [ ] **A 'tsc exit' check written as `| head; echo $?` or `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` always looks clean, and PIPESTATUS is empty under zsh** `testing`
  *Symptom:* Typecheck lines printed 'tsc: 0' next to a real TS2339 error; the replacement `echo EXIT: ${PIPESTATUS[0]}` printed a blank exit code that was read as success.  <br>*Cause:* $? after a pipeline is the exit status of head; PIPESTATUS is bash-only and the Bash tool runs zsh (use ${pipestatus[1]}).  <br>*Fix:* Run `npx tsc --noEmit` unpiped (or set -o pipefail) and gate on its own exit code; if filtering output, capture to a file first.
- [ ] **A hidden Browser pane gives blank screenshots and timeouts that were mistaken for page defects** `testing`
  *Symptom:* Screenshots of SkillSea, Hallalu and the finish verification came back blank or timed out with 'pane is not displayed'; a tab pinned to a local file preview refused navigation; the Aprizely board tab kept taking focus.  <br>*Cause:* A hidden pane does not composite frames, and a pinned tab cannot navigate.  <br>*Fix:* When the pane is hidden verify through DOM and text (find, read_page, get_page_text) and say so; open a new tab instead of navigating a pinned one; front the app tab before screenshots.
- [ ] **A syntax-check loop that pipes node --check through head reports ALL OK even when a file is broken** `testing`
  *Symptom:* `for f in *.js; do node --check "$f" 2>&1 | head -2 || ok=0; done` printed success for every file regardless of syntax errors, so the pre-deploy gate could not fail.  <br>*Cause:* In a pipeline the exit status is that of the last command (head, always 0), so `|| ok=0` and `&& echo` never see node's failure.  <br>*Fix:* Drop the pipe (run `node --check "$f" || fail=1`) or use `set -o pipefail`; make the loop exit non-zero and chain commit/deploy with && so a failing check stops the ship.
- [ ] **No tests anywhere in the project** `testing`
  *Symptom:* Every regression is found by a user; there is no signal before deploy that the core loop still works.  <br>*Cause:* AI-assisted builds move fast and produce working features without a test harness; nothing in the workflow demands one.  <br>*Fix:* Add at least a smoke test of the core loop (sign in → create → save → reload and it persists → export) and run it against the built artifact before shipping. Detector: TEST-NONE.
- [ ] **node --check rejects non-.js extensions and a check on its own line does not gate the commit and deploy** `testing`
  *Symptom:* `node --check src/private/deck.js.txt` threw ERR_UNKNOWN_FILE_EXTENSION, but because it sat on a separate line from the ship commands, the deploy shipped anyway with syntax never checked.  <br>*Cause:* node --check infers module format from the extension (.txt is unknown) and the guard was not chained with && to the ship commands.  <br>*Fix:* Copy to a temp .js and node --check that, and chain the whole ritual with && (or set -e) so a failing check stops commit and deploy.
- [ ] **Permanently skipped tests present as dead coverage** `testing`
  *Symptom:* The suite reports passing while meaningful cases are disabled, overstating real coverage.  <br>*Cause:* A failing test is skipped to unblock a release and never revisited.  <br>*Fix:* Fix it or delete it — a skipped test is not coverage. If it must stay, give it an owner and an expiry. Detector: TEST-SKIPPED.
- [ ] **simctl defaults write seeds the wrong domain: the iOS app never sees the injected token while read-back 'works'** `testing`
  *Symptom:* A seeded auth token read back correctly but the native app always showed the login screen.  <br>*Cause:* xcrun simctl spawn booted defaults write targets the simulator's global preferences, while UserDefaults.standard lives in the app's data container (Library/Preferences/<bundle>.plist).  <br>*Fix:* Resolve the container with simctl get_app_container booted <bundle> data, write the plist there, then relaunch so bootstrap() reads it. (A later hang was the disk-starved simulator, not the code.)
- [ ] **Smoke tests ran against the wrong app because a 200 from localhost:<port> was another project's dev server** `testing`
  *Symptom:* The Pixelbake Teams UI walk printed a tick for every screen with page titles 'undefined' and a console error from '[abba]'; elsewhere preview_start refused ports 8791 (joy-cloud) and 8812 (postplan) held by other chats.  <br>*Cause:* Fixed ports in launch.json are shared by concurrent sessions and the harness only checked HTTP status.  <br>*Fix:* Moved the harness to a free port (5231) and asserted a page marker (title or handle) before trusting any screenshot; check lsof -iTCP:<port> or use autoPort before starting.
- [ ] **Tests exist but no CI runs them** `testing`
  *Symptom:* A regression reaches production even though a test covering it exists, because nobody ran the suite.  <br>*Cause:* Tests are run manually and locally; no automation gates the push or deploy.  <br>*Fix:* Add a CI workflow that runs the suite on every push and blocks deploy on failure. Detector: TEST-NO-CI.
- [ ] **compose.mjs headless-Chrome render has no timeout and a shared profile, so one wedged render stalls a whole creative run** `perf`
  *Symptom:* Renders stopped on the second image in two sessions (600s tool timeouts, runs pushed to the background, leftover chrome-apz-compose processes); a carousel export using --allow-file-access hung until a fresh profile was used.  <br>*Cause:* execFileSync(CHROME, ['--headless=new', ...]) has no timeout or kill and reuses a default or fixed user-data-dir, so another session's headless Chrome or a stale profile lock makes the next launch hang.  <br>*Fix:* Fresh mkdtemp user-data-dir per render, a hard timeout (perl alarm because macOS has no timeout command), up to four retries, the target PNG removed before each try, own stale chrome processes killed first. Nine slides then exported in a loop with per-slide exit status.
- [ ] **Duplicate event listeners stack up on every re-render/re-mount** `perf`
  *Symptom:* A click/submit/keypress handler fires 2x, 3x, Nx (double submits, doubled network calls, runaway toasts); memory grows the longer the SPA session stays open and the tab gets sluggish.  <br>*Cause:* addEventListener (often on window/document) is called inside a render/mount path that runs repeatedly, with no matching removeEventListener/cleanup. Each pass adds another live listener that closes over stale state and never gets GC'd.  <br>*Fix:* Register global listeners once outside the render path, or in a mount hook with a symmetric removeEventListener cleanup; for dynamic lists use one delegated listener on a stable parent. Use a named function reference (not an inline arrow) so removal actually matches.
- [ ] **Worker subrequest limit (50 free / 1000 paid per request) — serial fan-out stalls or throws Subrequest Limit Exceeded** `perf`
  *Symptom:* A Worker that fans out many fetch()/KV/R2 calls in one request either stalls (serial) or throws 'Subrequest Limit Exceeded'.  <br>*Cause:* Each fetch/KV/R2 is a subrequest; too many concurrent overwhelm the event loop, too many serial blow latency. (Seen in Hallalu sitemap-candidate probing.)  <br>*Fix:* Batch/limit concurrency (Promise.all in capped chunks), cache, and keep per-request subrequests well under the plan limit.
- [ ] **Worker subrequest limit hit by per-row D1/KV/fetch in a loop** `perf`
  *Symptom:* A request that iterates over a list and does a DB/KV/fetch call per item suddenly throws 'Too many subrequests' (or the CRON/batch job dies partway) once the list grows; fine with small demo data.  <br>*Cause:* Every D1, KV, R2, DO, and fetch() call counts as a subrequest against the per-invocation cap. N+1 loops the AI generated blow through it under real load.  <br>*Fix:* Collapse N calls into one: batch D1 with db.batch()/a single JOIN, use KV bulk/list, cache repeated fetches; move genuinely large fan-out to a Queue or Workflow. Catch the limit exception so the job resumes rather than silently truncating.
- [ ] **No Open Graph or Twitter card — shared links render as a bare URL** `SEO`
  *Symptom:* Pasting the product link into a message, Slack or social post shows a naked URL with no title, description or image, so nobody clicks.  <br>*Cause:* og: and twitter: meta tags are never added because nothing in the page looks broken without them.  <br>*Fix:* Add og:title, og:description, og:image (1200×630) and twitter:card to every shareable page, and verify with a card validator. Detector: SEO-NO-OG.
- [ ] **No viewport meta — the page renders desktop-width on phones** `SEO`
  *Symptom:* On a phone the whole layout is zoomed out and tiny, or controls run off the edge of the screen.  <br>*Cause:* The viewport meta tag is missing, so mobile browsers assume a ~980px desktop viewport.  <br>*Fix:* Add <meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1">. This is also the root cause behind many 'control is off-screen on mobile' reports. Detector: SEO-NO-VIEWPORT.
- [ ] **Page has no <title> or meta description** `SEO`
  *Symptom:* Browser tabs read 'index.html' or the domain, and search results show a machine-generated snippet.  <br>*Cause:* The page is built as an app shell rather than a document; the head is never filled in.  <br>*Fix:* Give every page a unique descriptive <title> and a meta description written for a human reading a search result. Detectors: SEO-NO-TITLE, SEO-NO-DESC.
- [ ] **Later same-specificity CSS rule silently overrides mobile media query** `ui`
  *Symptom:* A mobile-only sizing rule had no effect; the element overflowed off-screen on phones.  <br>*Cause:* A global sizing rule appeared LATER in the stylesheet at equal specificity, so source order won over the earlier @media (max-width) override.  <br>*Fix:* Place responsive/mobile overrides after the global rules they must beat (or raise their specificity); verify computed width, not just that the rule exists.
- [ ] **LLM replies shown in plain-text surfaces carry literal ** markers and ```json fences** `ui`
  *Symptom:* Hallalu recap emails opened with '**Here's a quick recap:**' under a second recap header; Abba's Ask Ava showed asterisks around bold words; /api/ai with a JSON-only prompt returned text starting with a ```json fence.  <br>*Cause:* System prompts did not forbid markdown and the clients render model text through textarea or textContent.  <br>*Fix:* The recap prompt now demands plain text with no markdown and a single recap header; Abba added a small bold and italic renderer for Ava output; the client JSON extractor strips fences. The portal Ava draft path was not checked for fence handling.
- [ ] **Background research agents were killed repeatedly by 'computer went to sleep mid-response' and whole runs were lost** `other`
  *Symptom:* GMAT, GRE (twice), Phrases and a GRE top-up agents all failed with the same error; only one had saved partial output.  <br>*Cause:* macOS sleep during a long agent turn; agents wrote results only at the end.  <br>*Fix:* Run long agent batches under caffeinate -i, have agents append results to a file as they go (the GRE top-up kept 433 entries A-R that way), and resume from the partial file.
- [ ] **Browser-pane javascript_tool shares one global scope: re-declaring const across calls and top-level await both throw** `other`
  *Symptom:* Consecutive calls failed with 'Identifier c has already been declared' and 'await is only valid in async functions', wasting verification round-trips.  <br>*Cause:* Each snippet runs as a script in the same page global scope, so top-level const/let persists and top-level await is not allowed.  <br>*Fix:* Wrap every snippet in an IIFE, (async()=>{...})() when awaiting, and return JSON.stringify of the result.
- [ ] **Commands handed to the user's zsh failed twice: a [!.] glob hit history expansion and BSD du refused -s with -d while stderr went to /dev/null** `other`
  *Symptom:* The pasted 'sudo du -shx /Users/rhemajoy/* /Users/rhemajoy/.[!.]*' returned 'zsh: event not found: .]'; the replacement 'sudo du -shx -d1' returned nothing because BSD du rejects -s combined with -d and the error was discarded, costing three more round trips  <br>*Cause:* Commands were written for bash and GNU du, run in an interactive zsh on macOS, with 2>/dev/null hiding the failure  <br>*Fix:* For user-run commands avoid ! in globs, use find -maxdepth 1 or du -hx -d1 without -s, keep stderr, and write output to a file the agent reads rather than asking for a paste
- [ ] **Concurrent session commit swept another session's staged files (shared git index)** `other`
  *Symptom:* A commit contained unrelated files staged by a parallel Claude session.  <br>*Cause:* Two sessions shared one working tree and index; git commit takes whatever is staged.  <br>*Fix:* Stage explicit paths and use git apply --cached for hunks, check git status before commit, and prefer a worktree per session.
- [ ] **du run as the user reported ~/Pictures as 416 KB when it held 57 GB, so the home folder was measured at 36 GB instead of 89 GB** `other`
  *Symptom:* A disk-full cleanup chased a phantom 115-160 GB of 'root-owned' data through four wrong sudo commands before sudo du showed Photo Booth Library 36 GB and Photos Library 20 GB; /System showed 184 GB because the Data volume is firmlinked under it  <br>*Cause:* macOS privacy protection (TCC) makes du skip Photos and Photo Booth libraries when run from Terminal, and the scan discarded stderr so the skips were invisible; the reference note the session read was 45 days old and named a different culprit  <br>*Fix:* Run sudo du -hx -d2 on the home folder first, never trust a non-sudo total that does not reconcile with df, keep stderr visible, and ignore /System when summing the Data volume
- [ ] **Full-disk state is misdiagnosed: sealed-snapshot df, unwritable tool output and a blocking shell update prompt** `other`
  *Symptom:* Bash commands looked failed with no output; df showed 12Gi used and 0 free on a 228Gi disk; a terminal tab sat on an oh-my-zsh 'Would you like to update? [Y/n]' prompt; a read-only df was denied by the classifier as a false positive.  <br>*Cause:* The harness writes each command's output to a /private/tmp file, so ENOSPC makes every command appear to fail even when it ran. On APFS the sealed system snapshot makes df / meaningless. The oh-my-zsh prompt blocks that tab until answered.  <br>*Fix:* Check /System/Volumes/Data (5.7 GiB free while the snapshot showed 630 MiB), free regenerable caches first (npm, wrangler, pip), set DISABLE_UPDATE_PROMPT=true for agent terminals, and retry a denied read-only command with a different tool.
- [ ] **gh repo create --source=. ran from the wrong directory because the shell cwd resets between commands** `other`
  *Symptom:* A new GitHub repository was wired to the pitch folder instead of the project.  <br>*Cause:* The agent shell resets its working directory between calls, so a bare --source=. resolved against the old directory.  <br>*Fix:* Always cd inside the same command (cd project && gh repo create ...) or pass an absolute path, then verify git remote -v.
- [ ] **git filter-branch run from a repo subdirectory silently did nothing because stderr was discarded; 12 trailers survived in one repo** `other`
  *Symptom:* A four-repo loop rewrote three repos but PlannerStudio/webapp still showed 12 Co-Authored-By trailers on main; the loop printed no error.  <br>*Cause:* filter-branch requires the repo top level ('You need to run this command from the toplevel of the working tree'); PlannerStudio's .git is at ~/PlannerStudio, one level above the webapp folder the loop cd'd into, and the loop redirected 2>&1 to /dev/null.  <br>*Fix:* cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)" before filter-branch; verify with `git log main --grep` (not --all, which also counts refs/original backups) and never blanket-discard stderr on destructive history operations.
- [ ] **git push --force-with-lease rejected with 'stale info' after filter-branch -- --all, reflog expire and gc** `other`
  *Symptom:* All four rewritten repos failed to push main with '[rejected] main -> main (stale info)'.  <br>*Cause:* filter-branch --all rewrote the local refs/remotes/origin/* too, so the lease's expected value no longer matched the real remote tip.  <br>*Fix:* For these solo, user-authorised repos used plain git push --force and verified origin/main (0 trailers, same commit counts); in shared repos capture the old SHA first and use --force-with-lease=main:<old-sha>.
- [ ] **GNU timeout does not exist on macOS, so a guarded find scan returned nothing and read as 'no results'** `other`
  *Symptom:* `timeout 120 find ...` with stderr discarded printed no output; a reconciliation concluded there were no files until `which timeout gtimeout` showed neither exists.  <br>*Cause:* timeout/gtimeout are not installed on stock macOS and the command-not-found error was hidden by 2>/dev/null.  <br>*Fix:* Do not wrap commands in timeout on macOS (use perl -e alarm, or background+kill, or run plain); never discard stderr on discovery scans.
- [ ] **The definition-of-done skill committed and deployed but never pushed: eight commits sat unpushed until the user asked** `other`
  *Symptom:* On Hallalu a finish pass found eight unpushed commits on main (v264 to v271) after the work had been reported as finished; the user separately asked that /finish also push.  <br>*Cause:* The /finish ritual covered commit and deploy only, with no push step and no rule for repos that have no remote (SkillSea and Evertrue were local-only until a remote was created).  <br>*Fix:* The /finish description and checklist now say pushed to git; if there is no remote, say so and skip rather than leave a pushable commit behind. Evertrue was given a private GitHub remote.
- [ ] **Two sessions deploying one Worker from the same working tree shipped each other's half-finished edits** `other`
  *Symptom:* A live deploy contained another session's uncommitted changes and broke a route.  <br>*Cause:* wrangler deploy bundles the current directory, not a commit; concurrent sessions edit the same files.  <br>*Fix:* Deploy only from a clean, committed tree (or an isolated worktree with symlinked node_modules) and verify the pushed commit builds there first.
- [ ] **Unquoted URL containing ? aborts in zsh with no matches found and an empty fetch looks like a hash mismatch** `other`
  *Symptom:* curl on an unquoted URL printed zsh: no matches found, the empty body hashed to e3b0c442..., which was misread as a changed page.  <br>*Cause:* zsh treats ? as a glob and errors before curl runs; the empty-input SHA-256 then masqueraded as a real result.  <br>*Fix:* Always single-quote URLs, and treat e3b0c44298fc1c14... as the signal that the fetch returned nothing.
- [ ] **Unquoted zsh grep --include glob returns false negatives (searched nothing, concluded absence)** `other`
  *Symptom:* A search that should hit returned empty, leading an agent to declare a feature missing.  <br>*Cause:* zsh expands or errors on unquoted glob patterns (no matches found) so the argument never reaches grep as intended; an empty result was read as 'not there'.  <br>*Fix:* Always quote globs, prefer rg, sanity-check the search with a known positive, and never remove capability on a negative finding.
- [ ] **UTF-8 static HTML without a <meta charset> showed mojibake when served by python http.server (no charset header)** `other`
  *Symptom:* em dashes, middle dots and arrows rendered as garbled Latin-1 sequences in the preview  <br>*Cause:* server sent Content-Type without a charset and the page declared none, so the browser guessed wrong  <br>*Fix:* added <meta charset="utf-8"> as the first element of the head
- [ ] **Verification navigated the owner's pinned live-board tab away and the board log was lost from view** `other`
  *Symptom:* The owner had asked for the Aprizely live board to stay open; a demo check was loaded into that same Browser-pane tab and the owner complained.  <br>*Cause:* Checks reused the fronted tab instead of creating one.  <br>*Fix:* Open a dedicated verification tab (tabs_create, not foreground) and never navigate a tab the owner asked to keep open; restore the board URL if it was changed.
